## Attachments

Potential future Lean bindings, not new attachment claims: the strict calculus task covers the open comparisons_existsUnique, square and composition arguments and the already proved unit case; the realization task covers derivedSigma_existsUnique, realizationModels, scalarExtensionTowerCoherence and the model-association content of quotientModels. The first task is stated for arbitrary coefficient towers, so those existing l-adic declarations cover only its specialization.

## WANTED

None. All cited sources are available locally.

## What changed and why

The human explicitly returned this already reviewed item because its nine-step proof had 8309 characters of work in one item. No statement dispute was found and the parent statement was not changed. The only repair is distribution of this work into two independently provable statement-only tasks. The strict task handles former steps 3–7 (balanced sheaf maps, total signs, scalar square, reduction and identity/composition); the realization task handles former steps 1–2 and 8–9 (good models, simultaneous scalar association, normalized counits and identification of the specified limit maps). Neither new task states the whole item: the first applies to arbitrary ring-sheaf towers, without any completed operation; the second identifies completed maps and does not assert the scalar square or its unit/composition equations. The second task only needs the normalized finite maps, not the first task’s square or composition theorem, so the two proofs can start independently.

I read the current item’s entire old nine-step proof, its full statement and Lean record, the chapter record sec-smooth-purity, and the unit sk-def-purity-conv (which cites sk-def-sf-operations). For the strict task I read Stacks Tag 03EL, sites-modules.tex, lemma-tensor-product-pullback (lines 3648ff), including the bilinear-map adjunction proof, and Tag 07A4, sites-cohomology.tex, lemma-pullback-tensor-product (lines 3683ff), including its signed-total K-flat representative argument. These are constituents, not claims that Stacks states our entire strict-system theorem. The simultaneous sequence-map equations are expressly agent extractions of former proof steps 3–7.

For realization I read Stacks Tag 06YS, sites-cohomology.tex, lemma-K-flat-resolution (lines 3206ff) and Tag 0G7E, lemma-pullback-K-flat (lines 3507ff). The normalized completion and chosen-map identification is expressly the agent argument of former steps 1–2 and 8–9, not a quotation of a Stacks theorem. I also read the full statements of lem-pur-kflat-change-of-rings, lem-pur-kflat-total-tensor-model, lem-weil-pbc-ringed-pullback-preserves-kflat, lem-pur-coefficient-extension-reduction-model, lem-pur-completed-operation-tower-substitution, lem-coef-derived-system-evaluation-calculus, def-pur-integral-derived-operations, def-trf-integral-comparison-realization-models and lem-pur-integral-operations-scalar-extension. ## Search audit

Before creating nodes I searched the whole DAG for scalar/tensor, coefficient-square, comparison realization and the source tags, and ran blueprint_refs.py ancestor on the item and search on scalar tensor, coefficient square, quotient, realization, cochain, scalar extension, completed comparison, strict coefficient, normalized scalar and scalar comparison. None states either full new task. The close candidates are compared below. All Mathlib queries were run through the mandatory msearch.py command, with the two intended node ids fixed before searching; searches.jsonl contains the original results. Each node records its own queries exactly. A second semantic phrasing was used for both. Loogle queries with unqualified tensor, NatIso or the guessed Functor.LeftDerived name produced identifier errors; these are recorded as unsuccessful query results, not evidence of an exhaustive absence. The replacement strict query SheafOfModules.pullback returned actual pullback identity/composition declarations; semantic searches supplied the meaningful second search for the realization node.

I opened Mathlib/Algebra/Category/ModuleCat/ChangeOfRings.lean at the extendScalars_assoc declarations (1015,1034), Sheaf/PullbackContinuous.lean at pullbackComp and pullback_assoc (167ff), Sheaf/PullbackFree.lean at the named free-module comparison, CategoryTheory/Sites/Monoidal.lean at Sheaf.monoidalCategory (168), and RingTheory/AdicCompletion/AsTensorProduct.lean at ofTensorProduct and its naturality (59ff). They prove ordinary scalar associativity, ordinary pullback coherence, sheaf monoidal localization or ordinary module completion. They do not give the simultaneous signed strict-system comparison theorem or the fixed completed-operation counit equations. These are not Mathlib leaves.

## Review by Q06

I checked B–E of delivery-errors in full against the actual node statements and sources. Both new nodes introduce their own rings/sites/maps or prime/fields/schemes; every specialized notion is either defined inline or names an existing definition. They are unsplit with empty proof and proof_deps; their statements contain assertions and normalization equations, not proof sketches. New sources carry actual Stacks tags and labels but explicitly mark the additional agent argument. I left the parent statement and maintainer-owned fields untouched. The new statements are mathematically smaller tasks of different kinds, not a relocation of the whole theorem into one child.

Assembly step 1 uses the realization task’s explicit assertion that all these finite complexes are flat-term K-flat, its simultaneous balanced association and its normalized-system assertion. Its hypotheses are exactly the parent’s prime, finite extensions, invertible ell, morphism and good representatives. I checked its coefficient-extension identification against the full reduction-model statement: that statement really gives coherent counits, every transition, identity and composition, not just individual reductions.

Assembly step 2 uses the strict task’s stated chain-map equation, formulas and unit/composition equations. I read def-weil-integral-scalar-extension: its maps come from valuation-ring inclusions and commute with reductions even in the ramified case. I read def-weil-proetale-inverse-image: its ring map is the adjoint of the constant-section map. Both routes in a coefficient square send each constant section to the same coefficient image; the sheafification adjunction extends the equality. Thus the geometric-morphism and commuting-square hypotheses of the strict task hold. Its F_a and S_W^{ab} are precisely the finite operations, with the parent’s tensor convention changed only by the commutativity identification. A definition is used here only to specify maps, not as a substitute for a non-elementary comparison theorem.

Assembly step 3 uses both displayed counit equations and the limit-map conclusion of the realization task. The two equations are in the derived category of entire systems; I have not inferred equality of system maps just from evaluations. The only further cited theorem is lem-weil-pbc-ringed-strict-tower-homotopy-limit, whose statement really supplies the exact Rlim functor on strict towers over a single ring sheaf. First restricting to the completed L-coefficient sheaf makes its hypotheses hold. Preservation of equal composites, inverse pairs and identities is elementary functoriality. No assertion that tensor or pullback commutes with an arbitrary inverse limit is used. The exact quotations are in citations.json.

For small cases I checked zero complexes and zero coefficient rings (all maps remain the unique zero-module maps); identity coefficient maps (the action unitor, not literal equality of unassociated tensors); identity geometric morphisms (ordinary scalar extension); one-term complexes and homogeneous odd degree (no permutation and the same (-1)^p on both sides); unbounded complexes (direct sums, no infinite products in the total tensor); repeated indices and a quotient whose target is zero; and a ramified extension with e>1, where the same-index quotient map need not be flat. Every tensor association uses a ring map, never flatness. The generator in kappa’s inverse has an inverse-image B coefficient, which is mapped by phi^b; it is not treated as a target coefficient without that map. For E→L→F the coefficient factor is d*j(b), and reductions preserve that product and one. For realization I checked that the statement uses only normalized systems for invertible counits, and uses the K-injective-normalized whole-tower counit and fixed scalar counits, so uniqueness of arbitrary natural isomorphisms is never claimed.

No new proof step exceeds 2000 characters; the entire parent assembly is 1932 characters. The old long arguments have not been silently shortened: every old sentence is retained verbatim below and allocated to a new task. All new tasks have no proof steps by the explicit re-split instruction.

## Original argument allocation (unabridged)

The following text is copied exactly from the current repository item, including the historical Lean-status sentence in step 7. It is preserved as proof material for the workers who next split the two new nodes. It is not inserted as proof steps of those unsplit nodes.

### Former step 1 → lem-pur-normalized-scalar-comparison-realization

Apply [lem-pur-kflat-change-of-rings] to $R_{E,Y}\to A_{Y,m}$ and then $A_{Y,m}\to B_{Y,m}$: $P_m,Q_m,sP_m,sQ_m$ are flat-term K-flat complexes, without a flatness hypothesis on either ring map. By [lem-pur-kflat-total-tensor-model] their total tensors are also flat-term K-flat. Apply [lem-weil-pbc-ringed-pullback-preserves-kflat] to the finite ringed-topos pullbacks specified in [def-weil-proetale-inverse-image]; the pulled-back complexes are valid K-flat models as well. Thus every ordinary tensor and pullback used below has the stated derived interpretation.

Former uses: lem-pur-kflat-change-of-rings, lem-pur-kflat-total-tensor-model, lem-weil-pbc-ringed-pullback-preserves-kflat, def-weil-proetale-inverse-image.

### Former step 2 → lem-pur-normalized-scalar-comparison-realization

Use the actual coefficient maps of [def-weil-integral-scalar-extension]. Define $\alpha_m:(P\otimes_{R_{E,Y}}A_{Y,m})\otimes_{A_{Y,m}}B_{Y,m}\to P\otimes_{R_{E,Y}}B_{Y,m}$ by $(x\otimes a)\otimes b\mapsto x\otimes\iota_m(a)b$; its inverse sends $x\otimes b$ to $(x\otimes1)\otimes b$. The tensor balancing relations in [def-pur-sheaf-tensor-product] show that these are inverse sheaf maps. For $m\geq n$, reduction of the displayed image is $x\otimes\iota_n(\bar a)\bar b$, exactly the image after reduction first. They are therefore simultaneous chain isomorphisms of towers, also for $Q$. The normalization and coherent reduction identification for these very models are the assertions of [lem-pur-coefficient-extension-reduction-model], not an assertion that $A_m\to B_m$ is flat.

Former uses: def-weil-integral-scalar-extension, def-pur-sheaf-tensor-product, lem-pur-coefficient-extension-reduction-model.

### Former step 3 → lem-pur-strict-coefficient-comparison-calculus

Write coefficient factors on the left, using commutativity, and suppress inverse-image symbols on local sections. For each level, $\kappa_K$ is the scalar-association map $b\otimes(a\otimes x)\mapsto b\iota_m(a)\otimes(1\otimes x)$; the right-hand side is in $f_{L,m}^*(s_{Y,m}K)$. Its inverse is $b\otimes(d\otimes x)\mapsto b\,c^f_{B_m}(d)\otimes(1\otimes x)$, where the right-hand side is in $s_{X,m}(f_{E,m}^*K)$. Here $a$ is an $A_{X,m}$ coefficient and $d$ is an inverse-image $B_{Y,m}$ coefficient. The coefficient square in [def-weil-proetale-inverse-image] commutes with $\iota_m$: on a constant section both composites send it to its image in $B_m$, and the defining sheafification adjunction extends this equality. Tensor balancing [def-pur-sheaf-tensor-product] then verifies both inverse identities. These formulas are degreewise and use no differential on coefficients, so they define inverse chain maps.

Former uses: def-weil-proetale-inverse-image, def-pur-sheaf-tensor-product.

### Former step 4 → lem-pur-strict-coefficient-comparison-calculus

Apply [lem-pur-cochain-pullback-tensor-coherence] also to the identity inverse image with ring map $A_{W,m}\to B_{W,m}$. Its comparison is precisely $\sigma_W$, with formulas $b\otimes(x\otimes y)\mapsto(b\otimes x)\otimes(1\otimes y)$ and inverse $(b\otimes x)\otimes(d\otimes y)\mapsto bd\otimes(x\otimes y)$. Applied to $f$ with coefficients $A_m$ or $B_m$ it gives $c_E,c_L$. These are chain isomorphisms for direct-sum totals, including unbounded ones. In degrees $(p,q)$ their differentials have the two terms with coefficients $1$ and $(-1)^p$, respectively [def-pc-tensor-complex-sheaves]; the comparisons preserve $(p,q)$ and introduce no permutation, hence preserve this sign.

Former uses: lem-pur-cochain-pullback-tensor-coherence, def-pc-tensor-complex-sheaves.

### Former step 5 → lem-pur-strict-coefficient-comparison-calculus

Apply the coefficient-square clause of [lem-pur-cochain-pullback-tensor-coherence] to the commuting square of finite coefficient ring sheaves for $f$. Under the scalar-association maps from step 3, its equation is exactly the displayed equation of the item. Explicitly, start with a local tensor $b\otimes(x\otimes y)$ with $x,y$ homogeneous. The left route associates scalars, splits the tensor by $\sigma_Y$, then splits the pullback by $c_L$; the right route splits the pullback by $s_X(c_E)$, splits scalars by $\sigma_X$, then associates each factor by $\kappa_P,\kappa_Q$. Each gives $(b\otimes x)\otimes(1\otimes y)$. General coefficients in either factor are moved to $b$ by balancing. Such tensors generate the presheaf tensor products and the direct-sum terms, and equality persists on sheafification [def-pur-sheaf-tensor-product]. Thus this is equality of chain maps, rather than just equality of their localized classes.

Former uses: lem-pur-cochain-pullback-tensor-coherence, def-pur-sheaf-tensor-product.

### Former step 6 → lem-pur-strict-coefficient-comparison-calculus

For any quotient $m\to n$, write $\bar b$ for the coefficient image and $\bar x$ for the image of a section of the relevant quotient complex. The formulas in steps 3--5 commute with reduction: $\overline{b\iota_m(a)}=\bar b\iota_n(\bar a)$ for $\kappa$, and both orders for $\sigma$ or $c$ give $(\bar b\otimes\bar x)\otimes(1\otimes\bar y)$. The inverse formulas multiply coefficients, and $\overline{bd}=\bar b\bar d$, so they commute too. Naturality of inverse image and the transition-compatible ring maps of [def-weil-integral-scalar-extension] give the same formulas for sections before suppressing inverse-image notation. The cochain lemma [lem-pur-cochain-pullback-tensor-coherence] gives the transition compatibility for $c$. Consequently all maps and both routes are strict tower maps; the square and its inverse hold in the strict tower category.

Former uses: def-weil-integral-scalar-extension, lem-pur-cochain-pullback-tensor-coherence.

### Former step 7 → lem-pur-strict-coefficient-comparison-calculus

For $L=E$ identify $A_m\otimes_{A_m}K$ with $K$ by the action unitor: $a\otimes x\mapsto ax$. The formulas for $\kappa$ and $\sigma$ become the identity under these unitors. For $E\subset L\subset F$, let $j_m:B_m\to C_m$ be the coefficient map of [def-weil-integral-scalar-extension] for $L\subset F$; since all three maps are induced by the inclusions $\mathcal O_E\subset\mathcal O_L\subset\mathcal O_F$, the map for $E\subset F$ is $j_m\iota_m$. Identify $C_m\otimes_{B_m}(B_m\otimes_{A_m}K)$ with $C_m\otimes_{A_m}K$ by $d\otimes(b\otimes x)\mapsto d\,j_m(b)\otimes x$. The successive $\kappa$ formulas and the direct one multiply the same coefficients in the same order. For $\sigma$, both the successive route on $d\otimes(b\otimes(x\otimes y))$ and the direct route give $(d\,j_m(b)\otimes x)\otimes(1\otimes y)$. Balancing proves these equations on all sections. Reduction preserves $d\,j_m(b)$, so these are strict-system equations. The coherent reduction identifications used here have exactly this transitivity and identity compatibility by [lem-pur-coefficient-extension-reduction-model]. In Lean the scalar/pullback identity is proved by precomposing with the inverse action unitor, testing with the pullback adjunction unit, and applying the generator normalization of def-trf-integral-comparison-sequence-systems. The tensor identity uses [lem-pur-scalar-tensor-unit], specialized to the sigma field of Comparisons and its pure-tensor formula at coefficient one. The other obligations remain open.

Former uses: def-pur-sheaf-tensor-product, lem-pur-coefficient-extension-reduction-model, def-weil-integral-scalar-extension, def-trf-integral-comparison-sequence-systems, lem-pur-scalar-tensor-unit.

### Former step 8 → lem-pur-normalized-scalar-comparison-realization

Use [lem-pur-coefficient-extension-reduction-model] to identify the coherent reductions of the integral scalar extensions of $M,N$ with the actual $B_m$-models of step 2, using its canonical counit and simultaneous $\alpha_m$ maps. Apply [lem-pur-completed-operation-tower-substitution] to these normalized systems: their finite pullback and tensor systems are normalized, and their derived limits are the completed operations on the limits, naturally in system maps. This supplies the coherent reduction identifications needed for the targets of $\kappa,\sigma$. Apply the coefficient-extension reduction theorem also to the integral pullback and tensor inputs, using the same substitution theorem at $E$ to identify their reduction systems. Thus the sources are the scalar extensions of those integral operations. The definitions in [def-pur-integral-derived-operations] use these finite models and derived limits. On these identified systems, the canonical scalar-association comparison is precisely $\kappa$ for pullback and $\sigma$ for tensor, by steps 3--4. Their limits therefore realize the first two isomorphisms of [lem-pur-integral-operations-scalar-extension] with their canonical tensor-association normalization. No uniqueness of arbitrary natural isomorphisms, and no commutation of pullback or tensor with arbitrary inverse limits, is asserted.

Former uses: lem-pur-coefficient-extension-reduction-model, lem-pur-completed-operation-tower-substitution, def-pur-integral-derived-operations, lem-pur-integral-operations-scalar-extension.

### Former step 9 → lem-pur-normalized-scalar-comparison-realization

After restriction to the completed $L$-coefficient sheaf, all towers and maps above are strict towers over one sheaf of rings. Apply the derived-limit functor of [lem-weil-pbc-ringed-strict-tower-homotopy-limit]. The levelwise inverse tower maps give inverse maps on derived limits because a functor preserves compositions and identities. Step 8 identifies these with the canonical integral pullback and tensor scalar-extension isomorphisms. Applying the same functor to the strict square, unit equations and successive-extension equations preserves them. Hence their derived limits have precisely the asserted quotient representatives and transition compatibility, also in the ramified case.

Former uses: lem-weil-pbc-ringed-strict-tower-homotopy-limit.
